=== NOTES 6:58 | Sep–4 Nov 2026: International - treaties, agreements and developments in international organisations
## SUB 0 | WTO, IMF, Banks and Regional Bodies
[58:0:0] # WTO
[58:0:1] - **WTO MC14 (Yaoundé, Cameroon, March 2026)** ended without consensus; the **e-commerce moratorium** (no customs duties on electronic transmissions, in place since **1998**) **lapsed** (**30 March 2026**), for the first time in 26 years.
[58:0:2] - No multilateral extension followed. A group of co-sponsors has applied the moratorium **only among themselves since 8 May 2026**; on **1 September 2026** the co-sponsors (**25 entries**, incl. **US, EU, UK, Japan, Australia, Singapore**; India **not** among them) circulated a revised proposal for a **permanent plurilateral moratorium**, open to other members.
[58:0:3] > As of Sep–Oct 2026 the WTO-wide e-commerce moratorium stays lapsed; only a plurilateral group keeps it among themselves.
[58:0:4] TABLE head: WTO term (Agreement on Agriculture) | Subject
  [58:0:4.0] Amber Box | Trade-distorting domestic support; subject to reduction commitments
  [58:0:4.1] De minimis (within Amber) | Exempt up to 5% of production value (developed), 10% (developing, e.g. India)
  [58:0:4.2] Blue Box | Direct payments under production-limiting programmes (fixed area/yield or livestock numbers); exempt
  [58:0:4.3] Green Box | Minimally or non-distorting support (government services); no limit; incl. public stockholding for food security and domestic food aid
  [58:0:4.4] Article 6.2 (development measures) | Certain support in developing countries exempt from reduction
  [58:0:4.5] TRIMs | Trade-Related Investment Measures - goods only (not services); bars measures against GATT Art. III (national treatment) and Art. XI (quantitative restrictions)
  [58:0:4.6] TRIPS | Trade-Related Intellectual Property Rights
  [58:0:4.7] Dunkel Draft | Draft Final Act of the Uruguay Round, 20 December 1991, by GATT Director-General Arthur Dunkel; became the basis of the WTO
[58:0:5] > PYQ (APPSC Group 2): TRIPS of WTO relates to **intellectual property**.
[58:0:6] # IMF, multilateral banks and blocs
[58:0:7] - **IMF 16th General Review of Quotas** (approved **2023**): takes effect only when members with **85% of total quotas** consent; as of **29 Oct 2025** only **132 members with 72.78%** had consented; deadline extended (Nov 2025) to **May 2026**; still **not implemented (April 2026)**.
[58:0:8] - **India's IMF quota**: **SDR 13,114.4 million** = about **2.75%** of total; **voting share about 2.63%**; **8th-largest** quota holder. Votes = **250 basic votes + 1 per SDR 100,000** of quota.
[58:0:9] - **SDR basket** ("paper gold", weights from **1 Aug 2022**): **US dollar 43.38%, euro 29.31%, renminbi 12.28%, yen 7.59%, pound 7.44%**; reviewed every **5 years** - next by **end-July 2027**.
[58:0:10] - **Eurozone**: **Bulgaria** became the **21st** euro-area member on **1 January 2026** (rate **1.95583 lev = 1 euro**).
[58:0:11] - **NDB**: **Uzbekistan** joined on **5 June 2026** - **10th member**, **first Central Asian** member.
[58:0:12] - **AIIB**: **Colombia** approved as prospective member (**Nov 2025**), taking approved membership to **111**.
[58:0:13] # IMF and World Bank governance; ADB
[58:0:14] - **Pakistan** to **chair the World Bank and IMF Boards of Governors in 2027** (21 Oct 2026). **Board of Governors** = highest decision-making body; each member appoints a **Governor and an Alternate**; it has **no executive authority over daily operations**.
[58:0:15] - IMF and World Bank: born at the **Bretton Woods Conference, 1944**; both HQ **Washington DC**; IMF MD **Kristalina Georgieva**; World Bank President **Ajay Banga**.
[58:0:16] - **ADB** (2 Nov 2026): **$139.55 million** loan for the **Puducherry Integrated Urban Livability Improvement Project (PIULIP)** - **ADB's first project in Puducherry**. ADB est. **19 Dec 1966**, HQ **Manila** (Mandaluyong), President **Masato Kanda**, 69 members; **India a founding member**.
[58:0:17] - **Puducherry UT** = **Puducherry, Karaikal, Mahe and Yanam** (Kavaratti is Lakshadweep).
[58:0:18] # Other bodies
[58:0:19] - **World Bank Global Economic Prospects**: growth steadying, **led by South and Southeast Asia**.
[58:0:20] - **WHO (3 September 2026)**: validated **Timor-Leste** for **eliminating trachoma as a public health problem**; award presented on **8 September 2026**.
[58:0:21] - **Global Biofuels Alliance**: new **African and South American** members.
[58:0:22] - **ASEAN**: joint **maritime security protocol** - search and rescue (SAR) and **IUU fishing**.
[58:0:23] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.73 (IMF 1944 Bretton Woods, MD Georgieva; ADB 1966, Manila, 69 members, President Masato Kanda; AIIB), p.80 (WTO DG Okonjo-Iweala); Rocket Indian Economy Sheet #13 p.16 (Uruguay Round: TRIPS, TRIMS); app notes 6:58 sub 6 and sub 7
## SUB 1 | India-EFTA TEPA and India-Switzerland
[58:1:0] # TEPA (Trade and Economic Partnership Agreement)
[58:1:1] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [58:1:1.0] Signed | 10 March 2024
  [58:1:1.1] In force | 1 October 2025
  [58:1:1.2] Significance | India's first FTA with a bloc of four developed European countries
  [58:1:1.3] Investment target | USD 100 billion
  [58:1:1.4] Jobs target | 1 million direct jobs, spread over 15 years (not 1 year)
  [58:1:1.5] Market access | EFTA offers 92.2% of tariff lines = 99.6% of India's exports (Sep 2025)
[58:1:2] - **EFTA members (full list)**: **Iceland, Liechtenstein, Norway, Switzerland** - **none is in the EU**.
[58:1:3] - EFTA: established **1960** (**Stockholm Convention**); HQ **Geneva**.
[58:1:4] # 2nd India-EFTA Prosperity Summit 2026
[58:1:5] - Addressed by **PM Modi** and Swiss President **Guy Parmelin** at **Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi**; marked **TEPA's first anniversary**.
[58:1:6] - Theme: **"Building a USD 100 Billion Investment Partnership, Creating 1 Million Jobs in India"**.
[58:1:7] # India-Switzerland agreements
[58:1:8] - During Guy Parmelin's **3-day visit**: **migration and mobility**, **young professionals exchange**, transport technology, mobility systems, infrastructure.
[58:1:9] - Switzerland: capital **Bern**; currency **Swiss Franc**.
[58:1:10] > EFTA has four members and none is an EU member; TEPA jobs target is over 15 years.
[58:1:11] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 p.73 and Part 02 p.41, p.121 (TEPA signed 10 Mar 2024, in force 1 Oct 2025; US$100 bn, 1 million jobs over 15 years; Parmelin visit on first anniversary); app notes 6:58 sub 0
## SUB 2 | UNESCO Heritage Lists and the Anti-Doping Convention
[58:2:0] # India's UNESCO Tentative List (2026)
[58:2:1] TABLE head: Date | Entries added | Total
  [58:2:1.0] 4 Sep 2026 | Rangpur-Sivasagar Historic Ensemble, Assam (Ahom Kingdom; Rang Ghar, Talatal Ghar, water tanks); Colonial Penal Settlements of the Andaman Islands (incl. Cellular Jail); Nicobarese Cultural Continuity, Central Nicobar; Shekhawati Painted Haveli Towns, Rajasthan | 73
  [58:2:1.1] 29 Sep 2026 | Bagh Cave Paintings (MP; Buddhist murals, nine caves on the Baghni); Udayagiri Caves (MP; Gupta period); Chambal Valley temples (Bateshwar, Padavali, Kakanmath; Nagara style); Ginnorgarh Fort (Sehore, MP; Raj Gond capital); "Sites of Satyagraha" - serial transnational nomination with South Africa (7 Indian + 2 South African sites) | 78
  [58:2:1.2] 22 Oct 2026 | Baratang Karst-Mud Volcano Complex (Andaman and Nicobar Islands; limestone caves, active mud volcanoes); Andaman Subduction Volcanoes (Barren and Narcondam Islands); Sirpur Cultural and Archaeological Landscape, Chhattisgarh (Hindu and Buddhist temples, monasteries) | 81
[58:2:2] - **Tentative listing** = a **preliminary step before nomination**, not inscription. India still has **45 World Heritage Sites** (37 cultural, 7 natural, 1 mixed; 45th = **Sarnath, 25 July 2026**).
[58:2:3] - **World Heritage Convention:** adopted **1972**, ratified by India **1977**; India's first sites (**1983**): **Ajanta, Ellora, Agra Fort, Taj Mahal**. **ICOMOS** advises on **cultural** sites, **IUCN** on **natural** sites.
[58:2:4] - **Barren Island** = India's only historically active volcano. **UNESCO:** est. **16 Nov 1945**, HQ **Paris**. **ASI:** est. **1861**. Culture Minister **Gajendra Singh Shekhawat**.
[58:2:5] > Rocket dates the five-entry batch (total 78) to **29 Sep 2026**; the daily CA ran it on 22 Oct with the three Andaman/Chhattisgarh entries (total 81).
[58:2:6] # UNESCO International Convention against Doping in Sport
[58:2:7] - **29 Oct 2026:** India **re-elected Vice-Chair** of the Convention's **COP Bureau**.
[58:2:8] - Adopted **19 Oct 2005** (UNESCO's **33rd General Conference**), in force **1 Feb 2007**; the **only legally binding international treaty dedicated to preventing doping in sport**; **192 States Parties** - UNESCO's **second most widely ratified** convention; India ratified **7 Nov 2007**; COP meets **every two years**.
[58:2:9] - **WADA:** est. **1999**, HQ **Montreal**; **NADA India:** est. **2005**, New Delhi; **National Anti-Doping Act, 2022**.
[58:2:10] - **Clean sport MoU (28 Oct 2026):** **MYAS, Ministry of Education and UNESCO** - clean-sport values in schools, signed at NADA's **Clean Sport Conclave**; aligned with **Khelo Bharat Niti 2025**.
[58:2:11] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 p.82 and Part 02 p.163 (five Tentative List entries, 29 Sep 2026, total 78; Bagh, Udayagiri - Gupta, Chambal, Ginnorgarh - Raj Gond, Sites of Satyagraha India-South Africa); Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.164 (45 sites: 37/7/1; Sarnath 25 Jul 2026); daily CA (EM) 22 Oct and 29-31 Oct 2026; app notes 6:58 sub 7
## SUB 3 | New Trade Pacts and Economic Agreements (Oct-Nov 2026)
[58:3:0] # India-New Zealand FTA in force
[58:3:1] - Signed **27 April 2026**, **New Delhi** (negotiated within a year); **NZ Parliament passed** the legislation **16 September 2026**; **in force 20 October 2026** (Commerce Minister **Piyush Goyal**).
[58:3:2] - **100% elimination of duties** on Indian goods to NZ (textiles, leather, footwear, garments); **FDI of US$20 billion** expected over **15 years**.
[58:3:3] - Trade target: from **US$2.4 billion** to **NZ$7 billion by 2030** (aim at signing: double trade to USD 5 billion in 5 years).
[58:3:4] - **DGFT Tariff Rate Quotas** for **apples, kiwi fruit and Manuka honey**. NZ: PM **Christopher Luxon**, capital **Wellington**, NZ Dollar.
[58:3:5] - **New Zealand - women's vote:** the **Electoral Act** of **19 September 1893** made NZ the **first self-governing country** where all adult women could vote in parliamentary elections (first voted **28 Nov 1893**).
[58:3:6] # India-Mexico PTA talks
[58:3:7] - **31 Oct 2026:** **Terms of Reference** for a **Preferential Trade Agreement** signed in **Mexico City** (Commerce Secretary **Rajesh Agrawal**); covers market access, **Rules of Origin**, customs, **SPS, TBT**, trade remedies, dispute settlement.
[58:3:8] - Diplomatic relations since **1950**; Mexico currency **Peso**.
[58:3:9] # Industry and technology pacts
[58:3:10] - **Bharat Forge - Pratt & Whitney Canada** (Oct 2026): long-term **aerospace engine component** supply deal. Bharat Forge est. **1961**, HQ **Pune**; P&WC is a subsidiary of **RTX Corporation** (formed **2020** by merging **Raytheon** and **United Technologies'** aerospace businesses).
[58:3:11] - **IIT Hyderabad - Mie University (Japan)** MoUs, 16 Oct 2026, for **semiconductor research** (exchanges, joint research). IIT Hyderabad: **2008**, **Kandi, Sangareddy**; Mie University: **1949**, **Tsu City**.
[58:3:12] - **India Semiconductor Mission:** launched **2021** under **MeitY**; **Semicon India Programme ₹76,000 crore**; **silicon** = most used semiconductor.
[58:3:13] # India-Japan Joint Crediting Mechanism (JCM)
[58:3:14] - **17 Oct 2026:** JCM **Implementation Guidelines** launched in **New Delhi** - move to project implementation.
[58:3:15] - JCM = **Japan-initiated bilateral, project-based** carbon-credit mechanism for low-carbon technologies; launched by Japan in **2013**, **first partner Mongolia**; principle **MRV**; under **Article 6 (6.2) of the Paris Agreement** - not a multilateral UNFCCC mechanism.
[58:3:16] - **Paris Agreement:** adopted **2015 (COP21)**, in force **2016**; well below **2 °C**, pursue **1.5 °C**.
[58:3:17] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.121 (India-Japan JCM under Paris Agreement Article 6.2), p.122 (India-New Zealand NZ$7 billion trade target by 2030); daily CA (EM) 16-31 Oct 2026; app notes 6:58 sub 1 and sub 7
## SUB 4 | Countries and Regions in News
[58:4:0] # US-Denmark-Greenland security arrangement
[58:4:1] - Announced by **Donald Trump on 18 September 2026**: long-term **US military presence**; bars Russian/Chinese bases.
[58:4:2] - **Greenland's sovereignty not transferred** - it remains **self-governing within the Kingdom of Denmark**.
[58:4:3] - **Sensitive investments** in Greenland face **US security scrutiny**; domestic procedures pending.
[58:4:4] - Greenland: capital **Nuuk**; **world's largest island**; US base **Pituffik Space Base**.
[58:4:5] - Denmark: capital **Copenhagen**; currency **Danish Krone**; **NATO founding member (1949)**.
[58:4:6] # UK nations seek constitutional change
[58:4:7] - Joint memorandum signed in **Cardiff, 14 September 2026** by **John Swinney (Scotland)**, **Rhun ap Iorwerth (Wales)**, **Michelle O'Neill (Northern Ireland)**.
[58:4:8] - **Good Friday Agreement 1998**: Northern Ireland border poll mechanism. **Scotland 2014 referendum**: **55% remain, 45% independence**.
[58:4:9] # Russia and Algeria
[58:4:10] - **United Russia won 355 Duma seats**. **State Duma** = lower house, **450 seats**, **5-year term**; upper house = **Federation Council**.
[58:4:11] - **Algeria**: Parliament bill - **death penalty for deliberately setting destructive forest fires**; President **Abdelmadjid Tebboune**; capital **Algiers**; **Algerian Dinar**; languages **Arabic and Tamazight**; **largest country in Africa by area**.
[58:4:12] - **Exploration Museum, Húsavík (Iceland):** covers the **Apollo astronauts' geology training in Iceland (1965 and 1967)**, Viking exploration and Arctic explorers; its **Astronaut Monument** (2015) names the **32** Apollo astronauts trained there.
[58:4:13] - **Yabloko barred (Aug 2026):** Russia's **Supreme Court** annulled the federal party list of the anti-war **Yabloko** party (**10 August 2026**), so Yabloko was excluded from the 2026 State Duma election.
[58:4:14] # 'Badr' defence MoU
[58:4:15] - Signed **21 Oct 2026** by **Türkiye, Qatar, Indonesia and Pakistan**; **'Badr' = "Full Moon"**; joint military production, technology, training, R&D.
[58:4:16] - Capitals **Ankara, Doha, Jakarta, Islamabad**; **Türkiye joined NATO in 1952** (not 1949).
[58:4:17] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 p.123 and Part 02 p.120 (US-Denmark-Greenland defence agreement; Pituffik Space Base); app notes 6:58 sub 5 and sub 7
## SUB 5 | Conventions, Courts and Scientific Bodies
[58:5:0] TABLE head: Item | Key facts
  [58:5:0.0] 70th IWC meeting | Hobart, Australia; India reaffirmed science-based cetacean conservation
  [58:5:0.1] IWC | Under International Convention for the Regulation of Whaling (Washington, 2 December 1946); secretariat Impington, Cambridge (UK); commercial whaling moratorium since 1986
  [58:5:0.2] ICJ advisory opinion | Binding state obligations to cut greenhouse gases and protect small island states
  [58:5:0.3] Global plastics treaty | 175 countries finalised a legally binding treaty in Geneva
  [58:5:0.4] Singapore Convention on Mediation | More states ratified (UN Convention on International Settlement Agreements)
  [58:5:0.5] IPCC special report | Extreme-weather attribution; called for more adaptation finance
  [58:5:0.6] UN-Habitat | Est. 1978, full programme 2002, HQ Nairobi; SDG 11; New Urban Agenda - Habitat III, Quito 2016; World Cities Day 31 October
[58:5:1] > Treaty completed in Geneva = global plastics treaty, not the ICJ opinion.
[58:5:2] - **INTERPOL notices:** **Red** – arrest or provisional arrest of a wanted person with a view to **extradition**; **Yellow** – locate a **missing person**; **Blue** – collect more information on a person's identity, location or activities; **Black** – information on **unidentified bodies**.
[58:5:3] # Southern Indian Ocean Fisheries Agreement (SIOFA)
[58:5:4] - **India approved accession** (Union Cabinet, **14 Oct 2026**; reported 20 Oct) - opens fishing opportunities for Indian vessels.
[58:5:5] - Signed **7 July 2006 in Rome**; **in force June 2012**; conservation and sustainable use of fisheries in the **Southern Indian Ocean**.
[58:5:6] - Covers **fish, molluscs, crustaceans**; **excludes highly migratory species** (Annex I of **UNCLOS**).
[58:5:7] - Bodies: **Meeting of the Parties** (at least once a year), **Scientific Committee**, **Secretariat** (no "Compliance Tribunal").
[58:5:8] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.122 (India-SIOFA accession approved 14 Oct 2026), p.82 (INTERPOL HQ Lyon), p.88 (UN-Habitat Nairobi); app notes 6:58 sub 4 and sub 7
## SUB 6 | UN Bodies, the 81st UNGA and UN Missions
[58:6:0] # UNSC and Visegrád Group
[58:6:1] - **UNSC = 15 members**: **5 permanent**, **10 non-permanent** (**2-year terms**).
[58:6:2] - At the **first India-Visegrád Group Foreign Ministers' Meeting**, all **4 members** backed India's **permanent UNSC membership**.
[58:6:3] - **Visegrád Group**: formed **1991** at **Visegrád, Hungary**; members **Czech Republic, Hungary, Poland, Slovakia** (4 after Czechoslovakia split in **1993**).
[58:6:4] # UN Convention against Cybercrime
[58:6:5] - EAM **S. Jaishankar** signed it for India in **New York on 25 September 2026**, during the **81st UNGA**.
[58:6:6] - **First comprehensive global treaty on cybercrime**. Earlier (Feb 2026) India had held back over privacy and data sovereignty, as with the IFD, to protect policy space.
[58:6:7] - Adopted by **UNGA Resolution 79/243 (Dec 2024)**; opened for signature at **Hanoi, Oct 2025** (**72 signed**); binding **90 days after 40 ratifications**; secretariat **UNODC**.
[58:6:8] # UNGA declaration on sea-level rise
[58:6:9] - **81st UNGA unanimously** adopted a declaration protecting **continuity of statehood and maritime rights** of low-lying states and **SIDS** despite submergence.
[58:6:10] - Process under **UNGA Decision 78/558**; co-facilitators **Australia and Cabo Verde**; calls for **adaptation and loss-and-damage finance**.
[58:6:11] # UN mission in Somalia ends
[58:6:12] - **UNTMIS** (UN Transitional Assistance Mission in Somalia) **ended operations on 31 Oct 2026**, closing **over three decades** of UN political missions in Somalia; a **Special Political Mission** under **UN DPPA**; tasks passed to the **Federal Government of Somalia** and the **UN Country Team**.
[58:6:13] - UNTMIS created **2024** by **UNSC Resolution 2753**, replacing **UNSOM** (**2013, Resolution 2102**). Somalia: capital **Mogadishu**, **Horn of Africa**. UN SG **António Guterres**.
[58:6:14] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 p.184 (UN Cybercrime Convention signed at Hanoi; UNODC manages it), p.8; Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.122; app notes 6:58 sub 3 and sub 7

## SUB 8 | US PERM Suspension, H-1B and Green Card
[58:8:0] # News
[58:8:1] - US suspended firms from the **Permanent Labor Certification (PERM)** programme: **Cognizant, Infosys, TCS, Wipro, HCL Technologies, Capgemini, Microsoft, Adobe**.
[58:8:2] - Reasons cited: **fraudulent filings, wage undercutting, displacement of US workers** ("America First").
[58:8:3] - US claim: the firms had **>230,000 H-1B approvals** and **>100,000 labour certifications** since **2009**.
[58:8:4] # Comparison
[58:8:5] TABLE head: Basis | H-1B | PERM | Green Card
  [58:8:5.0] Nature | Temporary work status | Labour certification | Permanent residence
  [58:8:5.1] Authority | USCIS (+ LCA to DOL) | US Department of Labor | USCIS / State Dept
  [58:8:5.2] Duration | Up to 6 years | Certification valid 180 days | Permanent
[58:8:6] - PERM is **employer-filed** on **Form ETA-9089**; then **Form I-140** with USCIS. Filing date = **priority date**.
[58:8:7] - H-1B extension beyond 6 years if PERM/I-140 pending **365 days**; up to **3 years at a time**.
[58:8:8] # Visa backlog
[58:8:9] - **Sept 2026 Visa Bulletin**: India **EB-2 unavailable**.
[58:8:10] - **Oct 2026**: EB-2 India final action **1 Nov 2013**; EB-3 India **1 Jan 2014**.
[58:8:11] - **EB-2** = advanced degrees or exceptional ability; **EB-3** = skilled workers.
[58:8:12] > PERM is neither a visa nor a Green Card.
[58:8:13] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:58 sub 2 (Carpediem CA); not in Rocket CA 2026 Parts 01-04

  was extended until MC15 | was made permanent for all members | was extended to 2030 | *lapsed on 30 March 2026
  x: The moratorium, in place since 1998, lapsed on 30 March 2026; only a plurilateral group applies it among themselves.

[ff72e75b19f89] sub0
  Which of the following pairs (WTO term – subject) is correctly matched?
  Amber Box – minimally distorting support with no limit | Green Box – trade-distorting support subject to reduction | TRIMs – intellectual property rights | *Blue Box – payments under production-limiting programmes
  x: Blue Box covers payments under production-limiting programmes; Amber is trade-distorting, Green is minimally distorting, and TRIMs is about investment measures.
[f38f548dbf13f] sub0
  Under the WTO Agreement on Agriculture, what is the de minimis limit for developing countries such as India?
  20% of production value | *10% of production value | 5% of production value | 15% of production value
  x: De minimis support is exempt up to 10% of production value for developing countries and 5% for developed countries.
[f176e09025796] sub0
  The Dunkel Draft (20 December 1991), which became the basis of the WTO, was the Draft Final Act of which round?
  Doha Round | Kennedy Round | *Uruguay Round | Tokyo Round
  x: The Dunkel Draft was the Uruguay Round's Draft Final Act, prepared by GATT Director-General Arthur Dunkel.
[f6ef26033bcaa] sub0
  Public stockholding for food security falls under which WTO category of farm support?
  Amber Box | Blue Box | De minimis support | *Green Box
  x: The Green Box covers minimally distorting support with no limit, including public stockholding for food security.

  x: A Red Notice seeks the location and provisional arrest of a wanted person pending extradition.
[ga847204f737b] sub5
  On which date is World Cities Day observed?
  5 June | 8 September | *31 October | 10 December
  x: World Cities Day is observed on 31 October; UN-Habitat is headquartered in Nairobi.
[g15ef356670fe] sub5
  For what purpose does INTERPOL issue a Blue Notice?
  To arrest a wanted person for extradition | To locate a missing person | *To collect more information on a person's identity, location or activities | To seek information on unidentified bodies
  x: A Blue Notice seeks more information on a person's identity, location or activities; Red is for arrest, Yellow for missing persons, Black for unidentified bodies.

  Meeting of the Parties | Scientific Committee | Secretariat | *Compliance Tribunal
  x: SIOFA's bodies are the Meeting of the Parties, the Scientific Committee and the Secretariat; there is no Compliance Tribunal.

[ca230ac4ed67cf] sub8
  Which of the following statements regarding the US suspension of firms from the PERM programme is incorrect?
  The suspended firms included Cognizant, Infosys, TCS, Wipro, HCL Technologies, Capgemini, Microsoft and Adobe. | The reasons cited included fraudulent filings, wage undercutting and displacement of US workers. | *PERM is a visa that directly grants permanent residence to an eligible worker. | The firms had more than 230,000 H-1B approvals and more than 100,000 labour certifications since 2009, according to the US claim.
  x: The incorrect statement is that PERM is a visa granting permanent residence. PERM is a labour certification and is neither a visa nor a Green Card. The figures concerning H-1B approvals and labour certifications, as well as the stated reasons for suspension, are accurate.
[cacacb8fc505db] sub8
  Which of the following pairs (US immigration pathway – nature) is correctly matched?
  *PERM – Labour certification by the Department of Labor | H-1B – Permanent residence | Green Card – Temporary work status for up to 6 years | H-1B – Labour certification by the Department of Labor
  x: PERM is a labour certification by the Department of Labor. H-1B is temporary work status (up to 6 years) and a Green Card is permanent residence.
[ca0b6a91db95bd] sub8

  x: The assertion is false because EB-2 covers advanced degrees or exceptional ability. The reason is true because EB-3 covers skilled workers.

## SUB 1 | Defence Exercises (Oct–Nov 2026)
[64:1:0] - **[15 Oct 2026] India-UK naval exercise Konkan-2026**: annual bilateral exercise of the **Indian Navy** and **UK Royal Navy**, **8-15 October** off **Plymouth**, Britain; **Talwar-class** guided-missile stealth frigate **INS Trishul** took part; two phases: **Harbour Phase** and **Sea Phase** (air, surface, underwater warfare, helicopter cross-deck landings, firing drills, anti-submarine warfare); part of the **India-UK Comprehensive Strategic Partnership** and **India-UK Vision 2035**; Konkan initiated in **2004**; INS Trishul (**F43**) commissioned **25 June 2003**; Talwar-class frigates initially **built in Russia**; Plymouth in **Devon**, home to **HM Naval Base Devonport**; other India-UK exercises **Ajeya Warrior (Army)** and **Indradhanush (Air Force)**; UK capital London, currency Pound Sterling; Indian Navy HQ New Delhi, **Navy Day 4 December**.
[64:1:1] - **[18 Oct 2026] India-UAE exercise Mountain Guardian-2026**: **5-18 October 2026** at **Chaubatia Foreign Training Centre, Uttarakhand**; focus **mountain warfare**; **92 personnel, 46 from each side**; Indian **Bhairav Battalion** and **UAE Mountain Infantry Battalion**; **women officers and female combat personnel** took part.
[64:1:2] - **[19 Oct 2026] India-Russia naval exercise INDRA-2026**: **13-18 October** in **Vladivostok** and the **Sea of Japan**; **Shivalik-class** stealth frigate **INS Sahyadri**; Russian Pacific Fleet represented by corvette **Hero of the Russian Federation Aldar Tsydenzhapov**; Harbour Phase at Vladivostok, Sea Phase in the Sea of Japan; INDRA series launched **2003**; INS Sahyadri (**F49**) commissioned **21 July 2012**; Shivalik class built by **Mazagon Dock Shipbuilders, Mumbai**; Vladivostok = HQ of the **Russian Pacific Fleet**; Russia capital Moscow, currency **Ruble**.
[64:1:3] - **[21 Oct 2026] 5th India-Australia exercise AUSTRAHIND-2026** in Kerala: **19 October – 1 November 2026** at the **Foreign Training Node, Thiruvananthapuram**; theme **Joint Sub-Conventional Operations in Island and Coastal Environment** under **Chapter VII of the UN Mandate**; annual **Army** exercise launched **2022**; 4th edition **13-26 October 2025** at **Irwin Barracks, Perth**; other exercises **AUSINDEX (naval)** and **Pitch Black** (multinational air exercise hosted by Australia); Australia capital **Canberra**.
[64:1:4] - **[22 Oct 2026] Indian Army exercise "Akhand Prahar-II"** in **Jaisalmer, Rajasthan**: follows Akhand Prahar-I; conducted jointly by the **Black Mace Brigade** and **Battle Axe Division**; tested tactics, coordination, next-generation equipment and force multipliers; Battle Axe Division is under **Southern Command (HQ Pune)**; **Indian Army Day 15 January** (**K. M. Cariappa** became first Indian Commander-in-Chief in **1949**).
[64:1:5] - **[23 Oct 2026] 33rd India-Singapore naval exercise SIMBEX-2026** begins in **Visakhapatnam** (AP): under **Eastern Naval Command (HQ Visakhapatnam)**; opening onboard **INS Nilgiri** (**Project 17A** stealth frigate) and **INS Kamorta** (**Project 28** ASW corvette); India's **longest-running uninterrupted bilateral naval exercise**; started **1994** as **Exercise Lion King**; Singapore currency **Singapore Dollar**.
[64:1:6] - **[27 Oct 2026] US-Japan exercise "Keen Sword 27"**: across islands and waters of **southwestern Japan**; **largest joint field-level exercise** in their history; **biennial** multi-domain **Field Training Exercise** of the **Japan Self-Defense Forces** and **US Armed Forces**; **US-Japan Treaty of Mutual Cooperation and Security** signed **1960**.
[64:1:7] - **[28 Oct 2026] 11th India-Indonesia Special Forces exercise "Garuda Shakti 2026"**: **27 October – 6 November 2026** at **Banyuwangi, East Java**, Indonesia; India represented by the **Parachute Regiment (Special Forces)**; jungle operations, obstacle crossing, precision targeting, mountaineering, airborne assaults; **Samudra Shakti** = India-Indonesia **naval** exercise; Indonesia capital Jakarta, currency Rupiah.
[64:1:8] - **[29 Oct 2026] INS Sahyadri arrives in Busan** for the India-South Korea naval exercise: reached **Busan Naval Port on 26 Oct 2026**; for the **India-Republic of Korea Navy (IN-RoKN)** bilateral exercise on maritime cooperation and interoperability in the Indo-Pacific; INS Sahyadri (F49): **Shivalik-class**, commissioned **21 Jul 2012**, built under **Project 17** by **Mazagon Dock Shipbuilders Ltd, Mumbai**; India-South Korea ties upgraded to **Special Strategic Partnership in 2015**; South Korea capital **Seoul**, currency **Won**.
[64:1:9] - **[01 Nov 2026] First BIMSTEC Maritime Security Exercise**: **30 Oct – 3 Nov 2026**, hosted by India at **Vijayapuram, Andaman and Nicobar Islands**; **all seven members**: **Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Myanmar, Nepal, Sri Lanka, Thailand**; follows the **6th BIMSTEC Summit** (theme "**BIMSTEC: Prosperous, Resilient and Open**", **4 Apr 2025, Bangkok**); objective "**Translating Vision to Action**"; focus naval interoperability, maritime domain awareness, coordination in the **Bay of Bengal**; BIMSTEC est. **6 Jun 1997** by the **Bangkok Declaration**, HQ **Dhaka**, Secretary-General **Indra Mani Pandey (India)**.
[64:1:10] - **[04 Nov 2026] 5th India-US tri-service exercise "Tiger Triumph-2026"** in **Visakhapatnam**, AP: **HADR** exercise, **2-7 Nov 2026** at the **Maritime Warfare Centre**; conducted as a **Tabletop Exercise (TTX)**; Navy, Army, Air Force and Special Forces; first edition **November 2019** on the eastern coast; other India-US exercises **Yudh Abhyas (Armies)** and **Vajra Prahar (Special Forces)**.
[64:1:11] - **[04 Nov 2026] 9th India-Sri Lanka Coast Guard High-Level Meeting** in **Mirissa**, Sri Lanka: Indian side led by **ICG DG Paramesh Sivamani**, Sri Lankan by **DG Ruwan Rupasena**; focus on marine pollution response, maritime SAR and law enforcement; **ICG est. 1 Feb 1977**, formally inaugurated **18 Aug 1978**, HQ New Delhi, under **MoD**; **Coast Guard Day 1 February**; India and Sri Lanka separated by the **Palk Strait** and **Gulf of Mannar**.
[64:1:12] - **[03 Nov 2026] INS Sudarshini completes the 10-month "Lokayan-26" voyage**: sail training ship returned to **Kochi on 2 Nov 2026**; began from Kochi on **20 Jan 2026**; **22,000+ nautical miles** across Asia, Europe, Africa and North America; **17 port calls in 11 countries**; commissioned **27 Jan 2012**, built by **Goa Shipyard Ltd**; **Southern Naval Command (HQ Kochi)** is the Navy's **Training Command**; **1 nautical mile = 1.852 km**.

## SUB 2 | Defence Deals, Industry and Service Chiefs
[64:2:0] - **[27 Oct 2026] DAC approves procurement worth about Rs 2.12 lakh crore** (meeting on **26 October 2026**, chaired by **Rajnath Singh**): radars, **long-range loitering munitions**, **multi-role helicopters**; **Active Offboard Decoy Systems** for the Navy; **Mobile X-Band Doppler Weather Radars** for the IAF; about **82%** to be made domestically; **DAC est. 2001**; **Acceptance of Necessity (AoN)** is the first approval stage; **DAP 2020**.

[64:2:3] - **[04 Nov 2026] DAC approves Project-18A Next Generation Destroyers**: **in-principle approval** by DAC chaired by Rajnath Singh; **stealth guided-missile destroyers**, about **₹80,000 crore**; successors to the **Visakhapatnam-class (Project-15B, four built by MDL)**; to counter **anti-ship ballistic missiles**, warships and submarines; supervised by the Navy's **Warship Design Bureau** with **MDL and GRSE**; DAC est. 2001; **MDL est. 1934, HQ Mumbai**; GRSE est. 1884, HQ Kolkata.

[64:2:5] - **[15 Oct 2026] India-Czech Republic expand defence industrial cooperation**: agreed **13 October 2026** in New Delhi between Defence Minister **Rajnath Singh** and Czech First Deputy PM and Minister of Industry and Trade **Karel Havlicek**; supports **Make in India** and **Atmanirbhar Bharat**; through the **India-Czech Joint Defence Committee**; Havlicek's four-day visit included **Bengaluru and Mumbai**; Czech capital **Prague**, currency **Czech Koruna**; independent **1 January 1993** after the dissolution of Czechoslovakia; **NATO 1999**, **EU 2004**; ties elevated to **Strategic Partnership on Innovation** in **January 2024**; Make in India launched **25 September 2014**.
[64:2:6] - **[01 Nov 2026] IG Defence–MSA Global MoU (India-Oman defence)**: Indian startup **IG Defence** and Oman-based **MSA Global LLC** to jointly develop and locally manufacture defence systems; exchanged by **Bodhisattwa Sanghapriya** (Founder-CEO, IG Defence) and **Col. M.S. Atwal (Retd.)** (Chairman, MSA Global) at the **2nd India-Oman Defence Industry Seminar, Muscat**; covers **KAL** (long-range strike UAS, **1,000 km** range), **BAJ** (unmanned aerial interceptor against drones) and **JWALA** (counter-drone micro-missile); GCC est. 1981, HQ Riyadh, six members; Oman capital **Muscat**, currency **Omani Rial**.
[64:2:7] - **[02 Nov 2026] IAF Chief Ashutosh Dixit unveils "VAYU" vision**: priorities: restore **fighter squadrons to sanctioned strength**, self-reliance in weapons, tri-service coordination; **VAYU = Vigilance, Victory and Readiness; Aatmanirbharta; Yukti (innovation); Unity of Effort** (Army-Navy-Air Force jointness); stressed drones, long-range precision weapons, space, cyber and AI; took office as **29th IAF Chief on 1 Nov 2026**; "**JAI**" = **Jointness, Aatmanirbharta and Innovation**; **IAF est. 8 Oct 1932**.
[64:2:8] - **[02 Nov 2026] VISV-2026 defence conference, New Delhi**: **VISV = Vision, Innovation, Synergy, Values**; inaugurated by **MoS Defence Sanjay Seth** at the **Manekshaw Centre**; theme "**Showcasing Defence Capabilities, Strengthening Global Partnerships**"; organised with **DRDO** and **CENJOWS**; compendium of DRDO products released in **Arabic, Spanish and French**; **DRDO est. 1958**, HQ New Delhi; **CENJOWS est. 27 Aug 2007**, tri-service think tank under the **Societies Registration Act, 1860**.

## SUB 4 | Foreign Policy Doctrines, Groupings and Strategic Ports
[64:4:0] # Doctrines
[64:4:1] TABLE head: Doctrine | Origin | Feature
  [64:4:1.0] SAGAR | 2015, PM Modi | Maritime doctrine for the Indian Ocean neighbourhood
  [64:4:1.1] MAHASAGAR | March 2025, Mauritius | "Mutual and Holistic Advancement for Security and Growth Across Regions"; extends SAGAR to the Global South
  [64:4:1.2] Act East | 2014, East Asia Summit, Nay Pyi Taw | Upgraded Look East policy
  [64:4:1.3] Neighbourhood First | - | Priority to South Asian neighbours; used with MAHASAGAR

## SUB 7 | Neighbourhood and India-assisted Projects
[64:7:0] # Electricity Exports to Nepal
[64:7:1] - **Ministry of Power** approved exports to the **Nepal Electricity Authority** for **18 hours/day**, **13 September – 31 December 2026**, after **floods** hit Nepal's hydropower.
[64:7:2] - Total **up to 654 MW**: **600 MW** via **Muzaffarpur–Dhalkebar** 400 kV line + **54 MW** via **Tanakpur–Mahendranagar** 132 kV line.
[64:7:3] - Volume from **January 2027** to be reviewed in **December 2026**.
[64:7:4] # Hilsa Exports to Bangladesh – a Reversal
[64:7:5] - About **500 tonnes** of Hilsa exported to **Bangladesh** in two months via **Petrapole Land Port** (West Bengal); **150–200 t** more expected.
[64:7:6] - Catch largely from **Bharuch, Gujarat**, via the **Howrah** wholesale market: **reversal** of the usual Bangladesh→India trade.
[64:7:7] - Bangladesh **restricted exports from 2012**; since **2019** allowed about **1 month during Durga Puja**.
[64:7:8] TABLE head: Period | Permitted | Imported
  [64:7:8.0] Sept 2024 | 2,420 t | 577 t
  [64:7:8.1] Sept 2025 | 1,200 t | 150 t
[64:7:9] - Hilsa/Ilish: family **Clupeidae**; species **Tenualosa ilisha**, **T. toli**; **anadromous**; IUCN **Least Concern**.
[64:7:10] - **State Fish of West Bengal**; **National Fish of Bangladesh**; Bangladesh produces about **70%** of world Hilsa.
[64:7:11] - Rivers: **Padma, Meghna, Hooghly, Ganga, Godavari**; **Joda Ilish** (a pair), **Ilish Utsab**.
[64:7:12] # India-assisted Projects Abroad → Host

## SUB 8 | US Sanctions and Tariffs – Russia, Iran and India
[64:8:0] # Lindsey O. Graham Sanctioning Russia and Iran Act, 2026
[64:8:1] TABLE head: Point | Fact
  [64:8:1.0] Signed by | President Donald Trump
  [64:8:1.1] Tariff | Up to 100% on imports from countries buying large amounts of Russian crude/gas; in addition to other duties
  [64:8:1.2] Automatic? | NOT automatic
  [64:8:1.3] Applies to | After 30 days, the top 5 importers of Russian energy (last 12 months) or top 5 sanction-evasion facilitators
  [64:8:1.4] Iran sanctions | Extended 5 years to 2031
  [64:8:1.5] Presidential waiver | Needs written national-interest certification + report to Congress
  [64:8:1.6] Review | USTR review within 180 days
[64:8:2] > The 100% tariff is "up to" and **not automatic**; it covers only the named top-five categories.
[64:8:3] # India's Exposure
[64:8:4] - **Russia** supplied **51%+ of India's crude imports** (**July 2026**); oil above **$100/barrel**; **Strait of Hormuz** constrained.
[64:8:5] - **US ≈ 20% of India's goods exports**.
[64:8:6] - Earlier **25% punitive tariff** (Executive Order): the broader regime was **struck down by the US Supreme Court** in **February 2026**.
[64:8:7] TABLE head: US law | Tariff on India
  [64:8:7.0] Section 301 (Trade Act 1974) | 10% (forced-labour probe)
  [64:8:7.1] Section 232 (Trade Expansion Act 1962) | 50% on steel, aluminium, copper
[64:8:8] - India's exports to US: **+18% (Apr–Aug 2025)**; slowed to **3.8% (Apr 2025–Feb 2026)**.
[64:8:9] # US Eases Russian Diesel Sanctions
[64:8:10] - **Trump–Putin call: 9 October 2026**; US Treasury eased **Russian diesel** sanctions **until 7 April 2027**.
[64:8:11] - Supplies: **300,000 t immediately**, **500,000 t in Nov 2026**, then **1 million t**; extra **3 million t** proposed.
[64:8:12] - Aim: cut fuel prices before **US midterms (3 November 2026)**; criticised by **Zelenskyy**.

[70:3:8] # International Space
[70:3:9] - **Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope** – launched **30 Aug 2026** by **NASA** on a SpaceX rocket; **wide-field infrared** telescope at **Sun–Earth L2** (~1.5 million km); ~8,000 kg; 5-year mission; 1.4 TB/day, up to 20 PB.
[70:3:10]   - **Wide Field Instrument** = **300-megapixel infrared camera** (field 1.5× full Moon) + **Coronagraph**; processes images ~**1,000 times faster than Hubble**.
[70:3:11]   - Ground stations: **New Mexico, Australia, Japan**. Named after Nancy Grace Roman – **NASA's first Chief Astronomer**, "**Mother of Hubble**".
[70:3:12]   - Universe: ordinary matter ~5%, dark matter ~27%, **dark energy ~68%**. Hubble since **1990**; **JWST** launched **2021**, also at L2; 6,000+ exoplanets known.

## SUB 6 | ISRO Missions, Launches and Engines
[70:6:0] # EOS-05 (GISAT-1A) on GSLV-F17
[70:6:1] - Launched **4 September 2026** by **GSLV-F17** from the **Second Launch Pad, SDSC-SHAR, Sriharikota**.
[70:6:2] - **India's first imaging satellite from geosynchronous orbit**; images the subcontinent **every 30 minutes** (priority areas ~5 minutes) from ~36,000 km.
[70:6:3] - Mass ~**2,367 kg**; placed in **Sub-GTO** (perigee 170 km, apogee 28,934 km, inclination 19.28°, azimuth 104°) – **not directly in GEO**.
[70:6:4] - Uses: agriculture, land resources, environmental and weather monitoring, disaster management.
[70:6:5] - Unlike LEO / Sun-synchronous EO satellites, it watches the **same large area with very high temporal frequency** (not once in 24 hours).
[70:6:6] TABLE head: GSLV-F17 item | Fact
  [70:6:6.0] Flight number | 19th GSLV flight
  [70:6:6.1] Height / lift-off mass | 51.7 m / 420.5 t
  [70:6:6.2] Stages | 3; 4 m ogive fairing
  [70:6:6.3] GS1 | S139 solid core + 4 L40H liquid strap-ons
  [70:6:6.4] GS2 | GL40HT liquid stage
  [70:6:6.5] GS3 | CUS15 cryogenic (LH2 + LOX)
[70:6:7] - **GSLV Mk II** capacity: ~**2,250 kg to GTO, 6,000 kg to LEO**; first flight **18 April 2001**; indigenous cryogenic stage from **GSLV-D5 (January 2014)**.
[70:6:8] - **SDSC-SHAR**: Sriharikota, **Andhra Pradesh** – ISRO's principal launch centre; former name Sriharikota Range; named after **Prof. Satish Dhawan**.
[70:6:9] # Orbits – Comparison
[70:6:10] TABLE head: Orbit | Key facts
  [70:6:10.0] LEO | 160–2,000 km; one orbit 90–120 min; ~15,562 active payloads (4 Sep 2026); India has 22 operational LEO satellites (Apr 2026 ISRO report)
  [70:6:10.1] MEO | Galileo (Europe): 30 satellites
  [70:6:10.2] Geosynchronous | Period ~23 h 56 min; can be oval (elliptical) or tilted (inclined)
  [70:6:10.3] Geostationary | Circular, equatorial, zero inclination, ~35,786 km; period ~24 h; appears stationary – communications, weather, continuous regional monitoring; India has 31 GEO satellites
[70:6:11] - Every geostationary orbit is geosynchronous, but a geosynchronous orbit need not be geostationary.
[70:6:12] # Semi-Cryogenic Power Head Test Article (PHTA)
[70:6:13] - Hot test at **100% rated level = 200 tonnes thrust** (47% level = 94 t) at **ISRO Propulsion Research Complex (IPRC), Mahendragiri, Tamil Nadu**.
[70:6:14] - **9th hot test** in the series; **first at full rated level**; total **35 seconds**, but **only 5 seconds at full level**.
[70:6:15] - Validated switch from low-pressure start tank to medium-pressure run tank.
[70:6:16] - PHTA includes **all engine systems except the thrust chamber**.
[70:6:17] - **SE2000** – 2,000 kN-class engine powering the **SC120** semi-cryogenic stage; SC120 to replace the **L110 core stage of LVM3**; with uprated **C32** cryogenic upper stage it raises LVM3 payload.
[70:6:18] TABLE head: Type | Fuel | Engines/stages
  [70:6:18.0] Semi-cryogenic | Refined hydrocarbon + LOX | SE2000 / SC120
  [70:6:18.1] Cryogenic | LH2 + LOX | CUS15, C32
[70:6:19] # Oceansat-3 (EOS-06) Tracks El Niño
[70:6:20] - **EOS-06 / Oceansat-3** saw **weaker easterly winds (westerly reversals)** and **lower chlorophyll-a** in **June 2026** – a developing **El Niño** in the tropical Pacific.
[70:6:21] - Analysis by **NRSC (ISRO)** of April–June data, 2023–2026; combined **OCM-3 chlorophyll + scatterometer winds**; productive zone **contracted westward**.
[70:6:22] - Launched **26 November 2022**, **PSLV-C54**, Sriharikota; polar Sun-synchronous orbit; 3rd-generation Oceansat.
[70:6:23] - Payloads: **Ocean Colour Monitor-3, Ku-band Scatterometer (SCAT-3), Sea Surface Temperature Monitor, ARGOS**.
[70:6:24] - El Niño weakens upwelling → lower phytoplankton.
[70:6:25] # ISRO Partnerships
[70:6:26] - **TRISHNA** – **Thermal InfraRed Imaging Satellite for High-resolution Natural resource Assessment**; **ISRO + CNES** (France); launch next year on **PSLV**.
[70:6:27]   - Observes land-surface temperature, emissivity, surface energy balance; uses: water stress, water quality, **urban heat islands**, volcanic anomalies, geothermal resources, snowmelt, glacier dynamics.
[70:6:28]   - ISRO: 1969, Bengaluru, Department of Space; Chairman **V. Narayanan**. CNES: 1961, Paris; head Lionel Suchet.
[70:6:29] - **ISRO–ESA agreement** (in force since **January 2002**) **extended until 8 January 2032** – exchange of letters at the International Space Summit, Paris, **10 Sep 2026**; signed by V. Narayanan and ESA DG **Josef Aschbacher**; covers human and robotic exploration, space weather, sustainable space activities.
[70:6:30] - ESA: est. **1975**, HQ **Paris**, **23 member states**.
[70:6:31] - **RTG**: ISRO sought proposals (2021) for a **100-watt RTG**; NASA invited ISRO to its **Moon Base** programme (not Mars).

## SUB 7 | Semiconductors, Supercomputing and Quantum
[70:7:0] # Semicon 1.0 vs Semicon 2.0
[70:7:1] TABLE head: Item | Semicon 1.0 | Semicon 2.0
  [70:7:1.0] Approved | December 2021 | Union Cabinet, July 2026 (notified 31 Aug 2026)
  [70:7:1.1] Outlay | ₹76,000 crore | ₹1,27,500 crore
  [70:7:1.2] Result / focus | 12 units approved (>₹1.64 lakh crore): 1 silicon fab, 1 SiC fab, 1 GaN micro-LED display fab, 9 ATMP/OSAT; 5 in production; in Gujarat, Assam, UP, Odisha, Punjab, Andhra Pradesh | Six pillars: Design, Machines and Materials, Fabs, Packaging, Research, Talent; up to 40% capex for CMOS/silicon fabs (35% compound/display); first commercial fab by 2028

[70:7:4] - **DLI** – **9% of net sales, up to 5 years, max ₹30 crore** per application.
[70:7:5] - **SEMICON India 2026** – **5th edition**, Yashobhoomi, New Delhi, **17–19 Sep**; theme "Silicon to Systems: Building the Ecosystem"; inaugurated by PM Modi; by ISM with SEMI.
[70:7:6] - Data: India imports **90–95%** of chips; ~20% of global chip-design workforce; **~7% of semiconductor GCCs**; ~85,000 engineers trained.

[70:7:11] # National Supercomputing Mission (NSM)
[70:7:12] TABLE head: Item | Detail
  [70:7:12.0] Launched | April 2015; ~₹4,500 crore
  [70:7:12.1] Steered by / implemented by | DST + MeitY / C-DAC Pune and IISc Bengaluru

  [70:7:12.3] Build approach | Assembly → Manufacturing → Design & manufacturing
  [70:7:12.4] Backbone | National Knowledge Network (does not make servers; C-DAC designed Rudra servers)

[70:7:14] - World's fastest supercomputer (2026) ~**2.19 exaFLOPS**. tera = trillion, peta = quadrillion, exa = quintillion.
[70:7:15] # Quantum

[70:7:17] - **IIT Delhi (Prof Bhaskar Kanseri)** – conventional data + entangled photons through the **same 50-km fibre**.
[70:7:18] - **IITM–C-DOT Samgnya** – National Hub for Quantum Communication (Dec 2025, DST), IIT Madras Research Park; MoU with **Q-Clairvoyance** (11 Sep 2026).

## SUB 8 | Nuclear Power – on Earth and in Space
[70:8:0] # India's Nuclear Programme
[70:8:1] - AEC Chairman **Ajit Kumar Mohanty**: target **100 GW by 2047**; private investment ~**₹7.5 lakh crore** for ~**30 GW**; ~₹25 crore per MW.
[70:8:2] - **NPCIL**: 24 reactors (~9 GW); 17 under construction (13 GW); est. 1987, Mumbai.
[70:8:3] - India has ~**25% of world thorium**; **SMRs** (up to 300 MW) for steel, cement.
[70:8:4] - **Homi Bhabha's three stages**: **PHWR (natural uranium) → FBR (plutonium) → U-233 from Th-232**; thorium in monazite sands of Kerala, TN, Odisha, AP.
[70:8:5] - **SHANTI Act** (Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India) – allows **SMRs and micro reactors** (not fusion reactors).
[70:8:6] # Nuclear Power in Space
[70:8:7] TABLE head: System | How it works | Examples / use
  [70:8:7.0] RTG (Radioisotope Thermoelectric Generator) | Decay heat of plutonium-238 → electricity; no moving parts | Voyager 1 & 2, Curiosity, Perseverance
  [70:8:7.1] RHU (Radioisotope Heater Unit) | Heat only – no electricity | Keeps instruments from freezing
  [70:8:7.2] Fission reactor | Controlled chain reaction → much higher power | Lunar/Martian habitats
  [70:8:7.3] NTP (Nuclear Thermal Propulsion) | Reactor heats liquid hydrogen | ~twice efficiency of chemical rockets
  [70:8:7.4] NEP (Nuclear Electric Propulsion) | Electricity accelerates ionised propellant | Low thrust, very high efficiency
[70:8:8] - Radioisotope systems used since **1961**; Voyager launched **1977** (5-year mission), now >20 billion km away.
[70:8:9] - NASA (Aug 2025): lunar fission system ≥100 kW by Q1 FY2030. White House memo (Apr 2026): 20-kW reactors in orbit by 2028, on Moon by 2030. China & Russia: Moon reactor by mid-2030s for the **International Lunar Research Station**.
[70:8:10] - Pu-238 shortage: US restarted production in 2015 after ~3 decades.
[70:8:11] TABLE head: Instrument | Key point
  [70:8:11.0] Outer Space Treaty 1967 | Bans nuclear weapons/WMD in orbit – does not ban peaceful nuclear power
  [70:8:11.1] Liability Convention 1972 | Launching State absolutely liable for damage on Earth / to aircraft in flight
  [70:8:11.2] UN Principles on NPS 1992 | Safety, radiation protection, re-entry notification – only non-propulsive use
  [70:8:11.3] COPUOS–IAEA Safety Framework 2009 | Guidance – not binding
[70:8:12] - **Cosmos 954**: Soviet satellite re-entered over northern Canada (1978); settled 1981 for C$3 million.
[70:8:13] - **PRANIK** – **Pranos Fusion** (Bengaluru, 2024) plans **India's first private fusion reactor**; design by 2027; small tokamak **PRAGYA**; HTS magnets; ITER at Cadarache, France (India one of seven members); DAE 1954; Institute for Plasma Research, Gandhinagar. *(news 03 Nov 2026)*
[70:8:14] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:70 (subs 2, 10); Rocket CA 2026 has no line on these items
## SUB 9 | Weather and Earth Science
[70:9:0] - **51st Doppler Weather Radar** – **Jamadarpali, Sambalpur, Odisha**, **17 Sep 2026**; 250-km radius, 150 m resolution; Make in India.
[70:9:1] - Odisha's 3 DWRs: **Sambalpur, Gopalpur, Paradip**; covers parts of Jharkhand, Chhattisgarh, **north AP**, West Bengal.
[70:9:2] - DWR uses **radio waves** (not sound); updates ~every **10 minutes**; Doppler effect – Christian Doppler (Austrian).
[70:9:3] TABLE head: DWR band | Use
  [70:9:3.0] X-band | Localised thunderstorms, lightning
  [70:9:3.1] C-band | Rainfall, cyclone tracking
  [70:9:3.2] S-band | Deep coastal, long-range cyclone monitoring
[70:9:4] - **Mission Mausam** – approved **Sep 2024**, **₹2,000 crore** for 2 years; launched **14 Jan 2025** (IMD's 150th foundation day); by **IMD, IITM, NCMRWF** (MoES); 40–50 more radars planned.
[70:9:5] - **183rd Seismological Observatory** at **Kishtwar, J&K** – by Dr Jitendra Singh; data to **National Centre for Seismology** (MoES); J&K now has four (Jammu, Srinagar, Udhampur, Kishtwar); zones II–V (V highest); Richter = magnitude, Modified Mercalli = intensity. *(news 04 Nov 2026)*
[70:9:6] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 p.173 (Doppler Weather Radars); app notes 6:70 (subs 9, 10)
